Input

{
  "filePath": "/source/standards/karpathy-guidelines.mdc"
}

Output

<path>/source/standards/karpathy-guidelines.mdc</path>
<type>file</type>
<content>
1: ---
2: description: Behavioral guidelines to reduce common LLM coding mistakes. Use when writing, reviewing, or refactoring code to avoid overcomplication, make surgical changes, surface assumptions, and define verifiable success criteria.
3: alwaysApply: true
4: ---
5: 
6: # Karpathy behavioral guidelines
7: 
8: Behavioral guidelines to reduce common LLM coding mistakes. Merge with project-specific instructions as needed.
9: 
10: **Tradeoff:** These guidelines bias toward caution over speed. For trivial tasks, use judgment.
11: 
12: ## 1. Think Before Coding
13: 
14: **Don't assume. Don't hide confusion. Surface tradeoffs.**
15: 
16: Before implementing:
17: 
18: - State your assumptions explicitly. If uncertain, ask.
19: - If multiple interpretations exist, present them - don't pick silently.
20: - If a simpler approach exists, say so. Push back when warranted.
21: - If something is unclear, stop. Name what's confusing. Ask.
22: 
23: ## 2. Simplicity First
24: 
25: **Minimum code that solves the problem. Nothing speculative.**
26: 
27: - No features beyond what was asked.
28: - No abstractions for single-use code.
29: - No "flexibility" or "configurability" that wasn't requested.
30: - No error handling for impossible scenarios.
31: - If you write 200 lines and it could be 50, rewrite it.
32: 
33: Ask yourself: "Would a senior engineer say this is overcomplicated?" If yes, simplify.
34: 
35: ## 3. Surgical Changes
36: 
37: **Touch only what you must. Clean up only your own mess.**
38: 
39: When editing existing code:
40: 
41: - Don't "improve" adjacent code, comments, or formatting.
42: - Don't refactor things that aren't broken.
43: - Match existing style, even if you'd do it differently.
44: - If you notice unrelated dead code, mention it - don't delete it.
45: 
46: When your changes create orphans:
47: 
48: - Remove imports/variables/functions that YOUR changes made unused.
49: - Don't remove pre-existing dead code unless asked.
50: 
51: The test: Every changed line should trace directly to the user's request.
52: 
53: ## 4. Goal-Driven Execution
54: 
55: **Define success criteria. Loop until verified.**
56: 
57: Transform tasks into verifiable goals:
58: 
59: - "Add validation" → "Write tests for invalid inputs, then make them pass"
60: - "Fix the bug" → "Write a test that reproduces it, then make it pass"
61: - "Refactor X" → "Ensure tests pass before and after"
62: 
63: For multi-step tasks, state a brief plan:
64: 
65: ```
66: 1. [Step] → verify: [check]
67: 2. [Step] → verify: [check]
68: 3. [Step] → verify: [check]
69: ```
70: 
71: Strong success criteria let you loop independently. Weak criteria ("make it work") require constant clarification.
72: 
73: ---
74: 
75: **These guidelines are working if:** fewer unnecessary changes in diffs, fewer rewrites due to overcomplication, and clarifying questions come before implementation rather than after mistakes.

(End of file - total 75 lines)
</content>

read · completed

Input

{
  "filePath": "/source/standards/ponytail.mdc"
}

Output

<path>/source/standards/ponytail.mdc</path>
<type>file</type>
<content>
1: ---
2: description: Ponytail, lazy senior dev mode. Always pick the simplest solution that works.
3: globs:
4: alwaysApply: true
5: ---
6: 
7: # Ponytail, lazy senior dev mode
8: 
9: You are a lazy senior developer. Lazy means efficient, not careless. The best code is the code never written.
10: 
11: Before writing any code, stop at the first rung that holds:
12: 
13: 1. Does this need to be built at all? (YAGNI)
14: 2. Does it already exist in this codebase? Reuse the helper, util, or pattern that's already here, don't re-write it.
15: 3. Does the standard library already do this? Use it.
16: 4. Does a native platform feature cover it? Use it.
17: 5. Does an already-installed dependency solve it? Use it.
18: 6. Can this be one line? Make it one line.
19: 7. Only then: write the minimum code that works.
20: 
21: The ladder runs after you understand the problem, not instead of it: read the task and the code it touches, trace the real flow end to end, then climb.
22: 
23: Bug fix = root cause, not symptom: a report names a symptom. Grep every caller of the function you touch and fix the shared function once — one guard there is a smaller diff than one per caller, and patching only the path the ticket names leaves a sibling caller still broken.
24: 
25: Rules:
26: 
27: - No abstractions that weren't explicitly requested.
28: - No new dependency if it can be avoided.
29: - No boilerplate nobody asked for.
30: - Deletion over addition. Boring over clever. Fewest files possible.
31: - Shortest working diff wins, but only once you understand the problem. The smallest change in the wrong place isn't lazy, it's a second bug.
32: - Question complex requests: "Do you actually need X, or does Y cover it?"
33: - Pick the edge-case-correct option when two stdlib approaches are the same size, lazy means less code, not the flimsier algorithm.
34: - Mark deliberate simplifications that cut a real corner with a known ceiling (global lock, O(n²) scan, naive heuristic) with a `ponytail:` comment naming the ceiling and upgrade path.
35: 
36: Not lazy about: understanding the problem (read it fully and trace the real flow before picking a rung, a small diff you don't understand is just laziness dressed up as efficiency), input validation at trust boundaries, error handling that prevents data loss, security, accessibility, the calibration real hardware needs (the platform is never the spec ideal, a clock drifts, a sensor reads off), anything explicitly requested. Lazy code without its check is unfinished: non-trivial logic leaves ONE runnable check behind, the smallest thing that fails if the logic breaks (an assert-based demo/self-check or one small test file; no frameworks, no fixtures). Trivial one-liners need no test.

(End of file - total 36 lines)
</content>

Lazy code without its check is unfinished: non-trivial logic leaves ONE runnable check behind, the smallest thing that fails if the logic breaks (an assert-based demo/self-check or one small test file; no frameworks, no fixtures). Trivial one-liners need no test.\n\n(End of file - total 36 lines)\n</content>",

        "text": "---\ndescription: Ponytail, lazy senior dev mode. Always pick the simplest solution that works.\nglobs:\nalwaysApply: true\n---\n\n# Ponytail, lazy senior dev mode\n\nYou are a lazy senior developer. Lazy means efficient, not careless. The best code is the code never written.\n\nBefore writing any code, stop at the first rung that holds:\n\n1. Does this need to be built at all? (YAGNI)\n2. Does it already exist in this codebase? Reuse the helper, util, or pattern that's already here, don't re-write it.\n3. Does the standard library already do this? Use it.\n4. Does a native platform feature cover it? Use it.\n5. Does an already-installed dependency solve it? Use it.\n6. Can this be one line? Make it one line.\n7. Only then: write the minimum code that works.\n\nThe ladder runs after you understand the problem, not instead of it: read the task and the code it touches, trace the real flow end to end, then climb.\n\nBug fix = root cause, not symptom: a report names a symptom. Grep every caller of the function you touch and fix the shared function once — one guard there is a smaller diff than one per caller, and patching only the path the ticket names leaves a sibling caller still broken.\n\nRules:\n\n- No abstractions that weren't explicitly requested.\n- No new dependency if it can be avoided.\n- No boilerplate nobody asked for.\n- Deletion over addition. Boring over clever. Fewest files possible.\n- Shortest working diff wins, but only once you understand the problem. The smallest change in the wrong place isn't lazy, it's a second bug.\n- Question complex requests: \"Do you actually need X, or does Y cover it?\"\n- Pick the edge-case-correct option when two stdlib approaches are the same size, lazy means less code, not the flimsier algorithm.\n- Mark deliberate simplifications that cut a real corner with a known ceiling (global lock, O(n²) scan, naive heuristic) with a `ponytail:` comment naming the ceiling and upgrade path.\n\nNot lazy about: understanding the problem (read it fully and trace the real flow before picking a rung, a small diff you don't understand is just laziness dressed up as efficiency), input validation at trust boundaries, error handling that prevents data loss, security, accessibility, the calibration real hardware needs (the platform is never the spec ideal, a clock drifts, a sensor reads off), anything explicitly requested. Lazy code without its check is unfinished: non-trivial logic leaves ONE runnable check behind, the smallest thing that fails if the logic breaks (an assert-based demo/self-check or one small test file; no frameworks, no fixtures). Trivial one-liners need no test.",

324: +func setOVSEndpointID(iface, endpoint string) error {
325: +	out, err := exec.Command(
326: +		"ovs-vsctl",
327: +		"set",
328: +		"Interface",
329: +		iface,
330: +		"external_ids:"+ovsEndpointExtID+"="+ovsMapValue(endpoint),
331: +	).CombinedOutput()
332: +	if err != nil {
333: +		return fmt.Errorf("tag OVS interface %q as %q: %w (%s)", iface, endpoint, err, out)
334: +	}
335: +
336: +	return nil

339: +func ovsInterfaceNames(condition string) ([]string, error) {
340: +	out, err := exec.Command(
341: +		"ovs-vsctl",
342: +		"--format=csv",
343: +		"--no-headings",
344: +		"--data=bare",
345: +		"--columns=name",
346: +		"find",
347: +		"Interface",
348: +		condition,
349: +	).CombinedOutput()
350: +	if err != nil {
351: +		return nil, fmt.Errorf("find OVS interfaces: %w (%s)", err, out)
352: +	}
353: +
354: +	text := strings.TrimSpace(string(out))
355: +	if text == "" {
356: +		return nil, nil
357: +	}
358: +
359: +	return strings.Split(text, "\n"), nil
360: +}
361: +
362: +func ovsInterfaceDeviceMissing(iface string) (bool, error) {
363: +	out, err := exec.Command(
364: +		"ovs-vsctl",
365: +		"--data=bare",
366: +		"get",
367: +		"Interface",
368: +		iface,
369: +		"error",
370: +	).CombinedOutput()
371: +	if err != nil {
372: +		return false, fmt.Errorf("get OVS interface %q error: %w (%s)", iface, err, out)
373: +	}
374: +
375: +	return ovsDeviceMissing(string(out)), nil
376: +}
377: +
378: +// ovsDeviceMissing reports whether an OVS interface error column says the netdev is gone.
379: +// A live interface returns "[]".
380: +func ovsDeviceMissing(errorColumn string) bool {
381: +	errorColumn = strings.TrimSpace(errorColumn)
382: +
383: +	return strings.Contains(errorColumn, "could not open network device")
384: +}
385: +
386: +func ovsMapValue(value string) string {
387: +	if value == "" {
388: +		return `""`
389: +	}
390: +	for _, r := range value {
391: +		switch {
392: +		case r >= 'a' && r <= 'z', r >= 'A' && r <= 'Z', r >= '0' && r <= '9':
393: +		case strings.ContainsRune("-_.:@", r):
394: +		default:
395: +			return fmt.Sprintf("%q", value)
396: +		}
397: +	}
398: +
399: +	return value
400: +}

463: +func assertReattachKeepsOnePort(t *testing.T, endpoint string) {
464: +	t.Helper()

469: +		t.Fatalf("add bridge: %v", err)
470: +	}

472: +		_ = goOvs.New().VSwitch.DeleteBridge(bridge)
473: +	})
474: +
475: +	n := &ovs{}
476: +	if err := n.Init(&clabtypes.NodeConfig{ShortName: bridge}); err != nil {
477: +		t.Fatalf("init: %v", err)
478: +	}
479: +	ep := clablinks.NewEndpointBridge(
480: +		clablinks.NewEndpointGeneric(n, endpoint, nil),
481: +		false,
482: +	)

484: +		t.Fatalf("add endpoint: %v", err)
485: +	}

488: +	if err := n.AddLinkToContainer(
489: +		context.Background(),

492: +	); err != nil {

494: +	}

496: +		t.Fatalf("delete peer: %v", err)
497: +	}

499: +		t.Fatalf("peer delete left %v, want the stale port", ports)
500: +	}

503: +	t.Cleanup(func() { _ = netlink.LinkDel(secondPeer) })
504: +	if err := n.AddLinkToContainer(
505: +		context.Background(),
506: +		second,

508: +	); err != nil {

512: +		t.Fatalf("ports after reattach = %v, want 1", ports)

523: +func addVethPair(t *testing.T) (netlink.Link, netlink.Link) {

530: +		PeerName:  peerName,

534: +	}

547: +func dataPorts(t *testing.T, bridge string) []string {

5: 	"fmt"
6: 	"net"
7: 	"net/netip"
8: 	"slices"
9: 
10: 	"github.com/charmbracelet/log"
11: 	"github.com/containernetworking/plugins/pkg/ns"
12: 	"github.com/vishvananda/netlink"
13: )
14: 
15: // Endpoint is the interface that all endpoint types implement.
16: // Endpoints like bridge, host, veth and macvlan are the types implementing this interface.
17: type Endpoint interface {
18: 	// Deploy deploys this endpoint through its associated link.
19: 	Deploy(context.Context) error
20: 	GetNode() Node
21: 	SetNode(Node)
22: 	GetIfaceName() string
23: 	GetIfaceAlias() string
24: 	GetIfaceDisplayName() string
25: 	GetRandIfaceName() string
26: 	GetMac() net.HardwareAddr
27: 	GetIPv4Addr() netip.Prefix
28: 	GetIPv6Addr() netip.Prefix
29: 	String() string
30: 	// GetLink retrieves the link that the endpoint is assigned to
31: 	GetLink() Link
32: 	// Verify verifies that the endpoint is valid and can be deployed
33: 	Verify(context.Context, *VerifyLinkParams) error
34: 	// HasSameNodeAndInterface returns true if an endpoint that implements this interface
35: 	// has the same node and interface name as the given endpoint.
36: 	HasSameNodeAndInterface(ept Endpoint) bool
37: 	Remove(context.Context) error
38: 	// Activate brings this endpoint's interface up in its current namespace.
39: 	Activate(context.Context) error
40: 	// IsNodeless returns true for the endpoints that has no explicit node defined in the topology.
41: 	// E.g. host endpoints, mgmt bridge endpoints.
42: 	// Because there is no node that would deploy this side of the link they should be deployed
43: 	// along
44: 	// with the A side of the veth link.
45: 	IsNodeless() bool
46: 	// Setters for ifaceName and Alias
47: 	SetIfaceName(string)
48: 	SetIfaceAlias(string)
49: 	IsRuntimeDiscovered() bool
50: 	// GetVars returns the endpoint-level vars.
51: 	GetVars() map[string]any
52: }
53: 
54: // EndpointGeneric is the generic endpoint struct that is used by all endpoint types.
55: type EndpointGeneric struct {
56: 	Node       Node
57: 	IfaceName  string
58: 	IfaceAlias string
59: 	// Link is the link this endpoint belongs to.
60: 	Link     Link
61: 	MAC      net.HardwareAddr
62: 	randName string
63: 	IPv4     netip.Prefix
64: 	IPv6     netip.Prefix
65: 	Vars     map[string]any
66: }
67: 
68: func NewEndpointGeneric(node Node, iface string, link Link) *EndpointGeneric {
69: 	return &EndpointGeneric{
70: 		Node:       node,
71: 		IfaceName:  iface,
72: 		IfaceAlias: "",
73: 		// random name is generated for the endpoint to avoid name collisions
74: 		// when it is first deployed in the root namespace
75: 		randName: genRandomIfName(),
76: 		Link:     link,
77: 		Vars:     make(map[string]any),
78: 	}
79: }
80: 
81: func (e *EndpointGeneric) GetRandIfaceName() string {
82: 	return e.randName
83: }
84: 
85: func (e *EndpointGeneric) GetIfaceName() string {
86: 	return e.IfaceName
87: }
88: 
89: func (e *EndpointGeneric) GetIfaceAlias() string {
90: 	return e.IfaceAlias
91: }
92: 
93: func (e *EndpointGeneric) GetIfaceDisplayName() string {
94: 	if e.IfaceAlias != "" {
95: 		return e.IfaceAlias
96: 	}
97: 	return e.IfaceName
98: }
99: 
100: func (e *EndpointGeneric) SetIfaceName(ifaceName string) {
101: 	e.IfaceName = ifaceName
102: }
103: 
104: func (e *EndpointGeneric) SetIfaceAlias(ifaceAlias string) {
105: 	e.IfaceAlias = ifaceAlias
106: }
107: 
108: func (e *EndpointGeneric) GetMac() net.HardwareAddr {
109: 	return e.MAC
110: }
111: 
112: func (e *EndpointGeneric) GetIPv4Addr() netip.Prefix {
113: 	return e.IPv4
114: }
115: 
116: func (e *EndpointGeneric) GetIPv6Addr() netip.Prefix {
117: 	return e.IPv6
118: }
119: 
120: func (e *EndpointGeneric) GetVars() map[string]any {
121: 	return e.Vars
122: }
123: 
124: func (e *EndpointGeneric) GetLink() Link {
125: 	return e.Link
126: }
127: 
128: func (e *EndpointGeneric) GetNode() Node {
129: 	return e.Node
130: }
131: 
132: func (e *EndpointGeneric) SetNode(node Node) {
133: 	e.Node = node
134: }
135: 
136: func (*EndpointGeneric) IsRuntimeDiscovered() bool {
137: 	return false
138: }
139: 
140: func (e *EndpointGeneric) Remove(ctx context.Context) error {
141: 	return e.GetNode().ExecFunction(ctx, func(ns.NetNS) error {
142: 		brSideEp, err := netlink.LinkByName(e.GetIfaceName())
143: 		_, notfound := err.(netlink.LinkNotFoundError)
144: 
145: 		switch {
146: 		case notfound:
147: 			// The netdev may already be gone while its bridge port is not, which is how OVS
148: 			// keeps a port whose name is not the topology interface name.
149: 			return removeBridgePort(e.GetNode(), e.GetIfaceName())
150: 		case err != nil:
151: 			return err
152: 		}
153: 		log.Debugf(
154: 			"Removing interface %q from namespace %q",
155: 			e.GetIfaceName(),
156: 			e.GetNode().GetShortName(),
157: 		)
158: 		if err = netlink.LinkDel(brSideEp); err != nil {
159: 			return err
160: 		}
161: 
162: 		return removeBridgePort(e.GetNode(), e.GetIfaceName())
163: 	})
164: }
165: 
166: // moveEndpoint moves the endpoint's interface into dst's namespace and transfers ownership.
167: // The interface is left in the DOWN state; callers that need it active should follow up with
168: // Endpoint.Activate once all endpoints are in place.
169: func moveEndpoint(ctx context.Context, e Endpoint, dst Node) error {
170: 	src := e.GetNode()
171: 	if src == nil {
172: 		return fmt.Errorf("endpoint %q has no source node", e.GetIfaceName())
173: 	}
174: 
175: 	if dst == nil {
176: 		return fmt.Errorf("endpoint %q has no destination node", e.GetIfaceName())
177: 	}
178: 
179: 	if src == dst {
180: 		return nil
181: 	}
182: 
183: 	if !slices.Contains(src.GetEndpoints(), e) {
184: 		return fmt.Errorf("node %q does not own endpoint %q", src.GetShortName(), e.GetIfaceName())
185: 	}
186: 
187: 	for _, owned := range dst.GetEndpoints() {
188: 		if owned == e {
189: 			return fmt.Errorf(
190: 				"node %q already owns endpoint %q",
191: 				dst.GetShortName(),
192: 				e.GetIfaceName(),
193: 			)
194: 		}
195: 		if owned.GetIfaceName() == e.GetIfaceName() {
196: 			return fmt.Errorf(
197: 				"node %q already tracks interface %q",
198: 				dst.GetShortName(),
199: 				e.GetIfaceName(),
200: 			)
201: 		}
202: 	}
203: 
204: 	if err := ensureOwnershipAltName(ctx, e); err != nil {
205: 		return err
206: 	}
207: 
208: 	if err := moveLink(ctx, src, e.GetIfaceName(), dst); err != nil {
209: 		return err
210: 	}
211: 
212: 	if err := src.ReleaseEndpoint(e); err != nil {
213: 		return err
214: 	}
215: 	e.SetNode(dst)
216: 	if err := dst.AdoptEndpoint(e); err != nil {
217: 		e.SetNode(src)
218: 		_ = src.AdoptEndpoint(e)
219: 		return fmt.Errorf(
220: 			"endpoint %q moved but destination ownership update failed: %w",
221: 			e.GetIfaceName(),
222: 			err,
223: 		)
224: 	}
225: 
226: 	return nil
227: }
228: 
229: // Activate brings the endpoint's interface up in its current namespace.
230: func (e *EndpointGeneric) Activate(ctx context.Context) error {
231: 	return e.GetNode().ExecFunction(ctx, func(_ ns.NetNS) error {
232: 		link, err := netlink.LinkByName(e.GetIfaceName())
233: 		if err != nil {
234: 			return err
235: 		}
236: 		return netlink.LinkSetUp(link)
237: 	})
238: }
239: 
240: func ensureOwnershipAltName(ctx context.Context, e Endpoint) error {
241: 	return e.GetNode().ExecFunction(ctx, func(_ ns.NetNS) error {
242: 		link, err := netlink.LinkByName(e.GetIfaceName())
243: 		if err != nil {
244: 			return err
245: 		}
246: 
247: 		return addOwnershipAltName(link, e)
248: 	})
249: }
250: 
251: // HasSameNodeAndInterface returns true if the given endpoint has the same node and interface name
252: // as the `ept` endpoint.
253: func (e *EndpointGeneric) HasSameNodeAndInterface(ept Endpoint) bool {
254: 	return e.Node == ept.GetNode() && e.IfaceName == ept.GetIfaceName()
255: }
256: 
257: func (e *EndpointGeneric) String() string {
258: 	ifDisplayName := e.IfaceName
259: 	if e.IfaceAlias != "" {
260: 		ifDisplayName += fmt.Sprintf(" (%s)", e.IfaceAlias)
261: 	}
262: 	return fmt.Sprintf("%s:%s", e.Node.GetShortName(), ifDisplayName)
263: }
264: 
265: // CheckEndpointUniqueness checks that the given endpoint appears only once for the node
266: // it is assigned to.
267: func CheckEndpointUniqueness(e Endpoint) error {
268: 	for _, ept := range e.GetNode().GetEndpoints() {
269: 		if e == ept {
270: 			// since node contains all endpoints including the one we are checking
271: 			// we skip it
272: 			continue
273: 		}
274: 		// if `e` has the same node and interface name as `ept` then we have a duplicate
275: 		if e.HasSameNodeAndInterface(ept) {
276: 			return fmt.Errorf("duplicate endpoint %s", e)
277: 		}
278: 	}
279: 
280: 	return nil
281: }
282: 
283: // CheckEndpointExists checks that a certain
284: // interface exists in the network namespace of the given node.
285: func CheckEndpointExists(ctx context.Context, e Endpoint) error {
286: 	err := CheckEndpointDoesNotExistYet(ctx, e)
287: 	if err == nil {
288: 		return fmt.Errorf("interface %q does not exist", e.String())
289: 	}
290: 	return nil
291: }
292: 
293: // CheckEndpointDoesNotExistYet verifies that the interface referenced in the
294: // provided endpoint does not yet exist in the referenced node.
295: func CheckEndpointDoesNotExistYet(ctx context.Context, e Endpoint) error {
296: 	return e.GetNode().ExecFunction(ctx, func(_ ns.NetNS) error {
297: 		// we expect a netlink.LinkNotFoundError when querying for
298: 		// the interface with the given endpoints name
299: 		var err error
300: 		_, err = netlink.LinkByName(e.GetIfaceName())
301: 
302: 		if _, notfound := err.(netlink.LinkNotFoundError); notfound {
303: 			return nil
304: 		}
305: 
306: 		return fmt.Errorf(
307: 			"interface %s is defined via topology but already exists: %v",
308: 			e.String(),
309: 			err,
310: 		)
311: 	})
312: }
313: 
314: func moveLink(ctx context.Context, src Node, ifaceName string, dst Node) error {
315: 	return src.ExecFunction(ctx, func(_ ns.NetNS) error {
316: 		link, err := netlink.LinkByName(ifaceName)
317: 		if err != nil {
318: 			return err
319: 		}
320: 
321: 		if err := netlink.LinkSetDown(link); err != nil {
322: 			return err
323: 		}
324: 
325: 		return dst.AddLinkToContainer(ctx, link, func(_ ns.NetNS) error {
326: 			return nil
327: 		})
328: 	})
329: }

(End of file - total 329 lines)

27: // netlink metadata needed to verify veth link pairing.
28: func DiscoverOwnedInterfaces(
29: 	ctx context.Context,
30: 	node Node,
31: 	knownIfaceNames map[string]struct{},
32: ) ([]OwnedInterface, error) {
33: 	return DiscoverOwnedInterfacesFor(ctx, node, ownershipName(node), knownIfaceNames)
34: }
35: 
36: // DiscoverOwnedInterfacesFor discovers interfaces in node's namespace that
37: // carry ownership markers for ownerName. Parking nodes use this when the
38: // namespace and logical node differ.
39: func DiscoverOwnedInterfacesFor(
40: 	ctx context.Context,
41: 	node Node,
42: 	ownerName string,
43: 	knownIfaceNames map[string]struct{},
44: ) ([]OwnedInterface, error) {
45: 	var interfaces []OwnedInterface
46: 
47: 	err := node.ExecFunction(ctx, func(_ ns.NetNS) error {
48: 		links, err := netlink.LinkList()
49: 		if err != nil {
50: 			return err
51: 		}
52: 
53: 		for _, link := range links {
54: 			name := link.Attrs().Name
55: 			if name == "lo" || name == "eth0" {
56: 				continue
57: 			}
58: 			if knownIfaceNames != nil {
59: 				if _, known := knownIfaceNames[name]; !known {
60: 					continue
61: 				}
62: 			}
63: 			if !isOwnedBy(link, node, ownerName, name) {
64: 				continue
65: 			}
66: 
67: 			info := OwnedInterface{
68: 				Name:        name,
69: 				Index:       link.Attrs().Index,
70: 				MasterIndex: link.Attrs().MasterIndex,
71: 				Type:        link.Type(),
72: 			}
73: 			if info.MasterIndex != 0 {
74: 				master, masterErr := netlink.LinkByIndex(info.MasterIndex)
75: 				if masterErr != nil {
76: 					return fmt.Errorf(
77: 						"failed to discover master for %q: %w",
78: 						name,
79: 						masterErr,
80: 					)
81: 				}
82: 				info.MasterName = master.Attrs().Name
83: 			}
84: 			if info.Type == "veth" {
85: 				veth, ok := link.(*netlink.Veth)
86: 				if !ok {
87: 					veth = &netlink.Veth{LinkAttrs: *link.Attrs()}
88: 				}
89: 				peerIndex, peerErr := netlink.VethPeerIndex(veth)
90: 				if peerErr != nil {
91: 					return fmt.Errorf("failed to discover veth peer for %q: %w", name, peerErr)
92: 				}
93: 				info.PeerIndex = peerIndex
94: 			}
95: 
96: 			interfaces = append(interfaces, info)
97: 		}
98: 
99: 		return nil
100: 	})
101: 	if err != nil {
102: 		return nil, err
103: 	}
104: 
105: 	sort.Slice(interfaces, func(i, j int) bool {
106: 		return interfaces[i].Name < interfaces[j].Name
107: 	})
108: 
109: 	return interfaces, nil
110: }

112: func DiscoverOwnedInterfaceNames(

115: 	knownIfaceNames map[string]struct{},

123: 	for _, iface := range interfaces {
124: 		ifaceNames = append(ifaceNames, iface.Name)
125: 	}
126: 	sort.Strings(ifaceNames)
127: 
128: 	return ifaceNames, nil
129: }

131: // ValidateOwnedInterface returns an error when an existing interface is not
132: // owned by the requested logical endpoint. Missing interfaces are valid.
133: func ValidateOwnedInterface(ctx context.Context, node Node, ifaceName string) error {
134: 	return ValidateOwnedInterfaceFor(ctx, node, ownershipName(node), ifaceName)
135: }
136: 
137: // ValidateOwnedInterfaceFor validates an interface in node's namespace against
138: // a possibly different logical owner. Parking nodes use this when the
139: // interface remains owned by the original node.
140: func ValidateOwnedInterfaceFor(
141: 	ctx context.Context,
142: 	node Node,
143: 	ownerName string,
144: 	ifaceName string,
145: ) error {
146: 	return node.ExecFunction(ctx, func(_ ns.NetNS) error {
147: 		link, err := netlink.LinkByName(ifaceName)
148: 		if _, notfound := err.(netlink.LinkNotFoundError); notfound {
149: 			return nil
150: 		}
151: 		if err != nil {
152: 			return err
153: 		}
154: 		if isOwnedBy(link, node, ownerName, ifaceName) {
155: 			return nil
156: 		}
157: 		return fmt.Errorf(
158: 			"interface %q on node %q exists but is not containerlab-owned",
159: 			ifaceName,
160: 			node.GetShortName(),
161: 		)
162: 	})
163: }
164: 
165: func RemoveOwnedInterface(ctx context.Context, node Node, ifaceName string) error {
166: 	if node == nil || ifaceName == "" {
167: 		return nil
168: 	}
169: 
170: 	return node.ExecFunction(ctx, func(_ ns.NetNS) error {
171: 		link, err := netlink.LinkByName(ifaceName)
172: 		if _, notfound := err.(netlink.LinkNotFoundError); notfound {
173: 			return removeBridgePort(node, ifaceName)
174: 		}
175: 		if err != nil {
176: 			return err
177: 		}
178: 		if !isOwnedBy(link, node, ownershipName(node), ifaceName) {
179: 			return fmt.Errorf(
180: 				"%w: interface %q on node %q",
181: 				ErrInterfaceUnowned,
182: 				ifaceName,
183: 				node.GetShortName(),
184: 			)
185: 		}
186: 
187: 		if err = netlink.LinkDel(link); err != nil {
188: 			return err
189: 		}
190: 
191: 		return removeBridgePort(node, ifaceName)
192: 	})
193: }
194: 
195: // bridgePortDeleter removes a bridge port that can outlive its netdev. OVS ports created for an
196: // interface name Linux will not accept are named after the random netdev, so deleting the veth
197: // from the container side leaves the port behind.
198: type bridgePortDeleter interface {
199: 	DeleteBridgePort(string) error
200: }
201: 
202: func removeBridgePort(node Node, ifaceName string) error {
203: 	if node == nil || ifaceName == "" {
204: 		return nil
205: 	}
206: 
207: 	cleaner, ok := node.(bridgePortDeleter)
208: 	if !ok {
209: 		return nil
210: 	}
211: 
212: 	return cleaner.DeleteBridgePort(ifaceName)
213: }
214: 
215: // isOwnedBy reports whether link carries the ownership marker of the logical
216: // endpoint ownerName:ifaceName. Interfaces of mgmt-net links deployed by earlier
217: // releases carry a marker for the bridge name of the mgmt-net link node instead
218: // of the stable mgmt-net owner name, and are accepted as well.
219: func isOwnedBy(link netlink.Link, node Node, ownerName, ifaceName string) bool {
220: 	if HasOwnershipAltNameFor(link, ownerName, ifaceName) {
221: 		return true
222: 	}
223: 
224: 	if _, ok := node.(*mgmtBridgeLinkNode); ok && ownerName == mgmtNetOwnerName {
225: 		return HasOwnershipAltNameFor(link, node.GetShortName(), ifaceName)
226: 	}
227: 
228: 	return false
229: }

30: // clabAutoPort matches the random netdev name used when the topology interface name is not a
31: // valid Linux name. Those netdevs are what Open vSwitch records as the port name.
32: var clabAutoPort = regexp.MustCompile(`^clab-[0-9a-f]{8}$`)
33: 
34: var kindNames = []string{"ovs-bridge"}
35: 
36: // Register registers the node in the NodeRegistry.
37: func Register(r *clabnodes.NodeRegistry) {
38: 	r.Register(kindNames, func() clabnodes.Node {
39: 		return new(ovs)
40: 	}, nil)
41: }

43: type ovs struct {
44: 	clabnodes.DefaultNode
45: }
46: 
47: func (n *ovs) Init(cfg *clabtypes.NodeConfig, opts ...clabnodes.NodeOption) error {
48: 	// Init DefaultNode
49: 	n.DefaultNode = *clabnodes.NewDefaultNode(n)
50: 
51: 	n.Cfg = cfg
52: 	for _, o := range opts {
53: 		o(n)
54: 	}
55: 
56: 	n.Cfg.IsRootNamespaceBased = true
57: 
58: 	return nil
59: }
60: 
61: func (n *ovs) CheckDeploymentConditions(_ context.Context) error {
62: 	// check if ovs bridge exists
63: 	c := goOvs.New()
64: 
65: 	// We were previously doing c.VSwitch.Get.Bridge() but it doesn't work
66: 	// when the bridge has a protocol version higher than 1.0
67: 	// So listing the bridges is safer
68: 	bridges, err := c.VSwitch.ListBridges()
69: 	if err != nil {
70: 		return fmt.Errorf("error while listing ovs bridges: %v", err)
71: 	}
72: 	if !clabinternalslices.Contains(bridges, n.Cfg.ShortName) {
73: 		return fmt.Errorf("could not find ovs bridge %q", n.Cfg.ShortName)
74: 	}
75: 
76: 	return nil
77: }
78: 
79: func (n *ovs) Deploy(_ context.Context, _ *clabnodes.DeployParams) error {
80: 	n.SetState(clabnodesstate.Deployed)
81: 	return nil
82: }
83: 
84: func (*ovs) PullImage(_ context.Context) error             { return nil }
85: func (*ovs) GetImages(_ context.Context) map[string]string { return map[string]string{} }
86: 
87: func (n *ovs) Delete(_ context.Context) error {
88: 	for _, ep := range n.GetEndpoints() {
89: 		if err := n.DeleteBridgePort(ep.GetIfaceName()); err != nil {
90: 			log.Errorf(
91: 				"Could not remove OVS port %q from bridge %q: %v",
92: 				ep.GetIfaceName(),
93: 				n.Config().ShortName,
94: 				err,
95: 			)
96: 		}
97: 	}
98: 
99: 	// Ports created before they were tagged, or whose netdev disappeared with the container,
100: 	// are not named after the topology interface. Drop those leftovers too.
101: 	if err := n.removeMissingAutoPorts(); err != nil {
102: 		log.Errorf(
103: 			"Could not remove stale OVS ports from bridge %q: %v",
104: 			n.Config().ShortName,
105: 			err,
106: 		)
107: 	}
108: 
109: 	return nil
110: }
111: 
112: // DeleteBridgePort removes the OVS port for a topology interface. The port name matches the
113: // topology name only when that name is a valid Linux interface name. Otherwise Open vSwitch keeps
114: // the random clab-* netdev name, which has to be found via the tag recorded at attach time.
115: func (n *ovs) DeleteBridgePort(endpoint string) error {
116: 	if err := n.removeEndpointPorts(endpoint); err != nil {
117: 		return err
118: 	}
119: 
120: 	return n.removeMissingAutoPorts()
121: }
122: 
123: func (*ovs) DeleteNetnsSymlink() (err error) { return nil }
124: 
125: // UpdateConfigWithRuntimeInfo is a noop for bridges.
126: func (*ovs) UpdateConfigWithRuntimeInfo(_ context.Context) error { return nil }
127: 
128: // GetContainers is a noop for bridges.
129: func (*ovs) GetContainers(_ context.Context) ([]clabruntime.GenericContainer, error) {
130: 	return nil, nil
131: }
132: 
133: // RunExec is noop for ovs kind.
134: func (n *ovs) RunExec(_ context.Context, _ *clabexec.ExecCmd) (*clabexec.ExecResult, error) {
135: 	log.Warnf("Exec operation is not implemented for kind %q", n.Config().Kind)
136: 
137: 	return nil, clabexec.ErrRunExecNotSupported
138: }

140: func (n *ovs) AddLinkToContainer(
141: 	ctx context.Context,
142: 	link netlink.Link,

145: 	// retrieve the namespace handle
146: 	curNamespace, err := ns.GetCurrentNS()
147: 	if err != nil {
148: 		return err
149: 	}
150: 
151: 	// execute the given function
152: 	err = curNamespace.Do(f)
153: 	if err != nil {
154: 		return err
155: 	}
156: 
157: 	c := goOvs.New()
158: 
159: 	// need to reread the link, due to the changed the function f might have applied to
160: 	// the link. Usually it renames the link to its final name.
161: 	link, err = netlink.LinkByIndex(link.Attrs().Index)
162: 	if err != nil {
163: 		return err
164: 	}
165: 
166: 	logical := n.logicalEndpointName(link)
167: 	// A previous attach of this same topology interface may still be registered under the random
168: 	// netdev name. Remove it before adding the new one so redeploy does not accumulate ports.
169: 	if err := n.DeleteBridgePort(logical); err != nil {
170: 		return err
171: 	}
172: 
173: 	if err := c.VSwitch.AddPort(n.Cfg.ShortName, link.Attrs().Name); err != nil {
174: 		return err
175: 	}
176: 
177: 	if logical == "" || logical == link.Attrs().Name {
178: 		return nil
179: 	}
180: 
181: 	return setOVSEndpointID(link.Attrs().Name, logical)
182: }
183: 
184: func (m *ovs) GetLinkEndpointType() clablinks.LinkEndpointType {
185: 	return clablinks.LinkEndpointTypeBridge
186: }
187: 
188: // logicalEndpointName is the topology interface name for link. When that name is not a valid
189: // Linux interface name, the netdev keeps a random clab-* name and the topology name is an altname.
190: func (n *ovs) logicalEndpointName(link netlink.Link) string {
191: 	if link == nil {
192: 		return ""
193: 	}
194: 
195: 	name := link.Attrs().Name
196: 	alts := make(map[string]struct{}, len(link.Attrs().AltNames))
197: 	for _, alt := range link.Attrs().AltNames {
198: 		alts[alt] = struct{}{}
199: 	}
200: 
201: 	for _, ep := range n.GetEndpoints() {
202: 		iface := ep.GetIfaceName()
203: 		if iface == name {
204: 			return iface
205: 		}
206: 		if _, ok := alts[clabutils.SanitizeInterfaceName(iface)]; ok {
207: 			return iface
208: 		}
209: 	}
210: 
211: 	return name
212: }
213: 
214: func (n *ovs) removeEndpointPorts(endpoint string) error {
215: 	if endpoint == "" || n.Cfg == nil || n.Cfg.ShortName == "" {
216: 		return nil
217: 	}
218: 
219: 	c := goOvs.New()
220: 	// Valid Linux names are used as the OVS port name. --if-exists covers a missing port.
221: 	if err := c.VSwitch.DeletePort(n.Cfg.ShortName, endpoint); err != nil {
222: 		return err
223: 	}
224: 
225: 	names, err := ovsInterfaceNames(
226: 		"external_ids:" + ovsEndpointExtID + "=" + ovsMapValue(endpoint),
227: 	)
228: 	if err != nil {
229: 		return err
230: 	}
231: 
232: 	onBridge, err := bridgePortSet(c, n.Cfg.ShortName)
233: 	if err != nil {
234: 		return err
235: 	}
236: 
237: 	for _, name := range names {
238: 		if _, ok := onBridge[name]; !ok {
239: 			continue
240: 		}
241: 		if err := c.VSwitch.DeletePort(n.Cfg.ShortName, name); err != nil {
242: 			return err
243: 		}
244: 	}
245: 
246: 	return nil
247: }
248: 
249: // removeMissingAutoPorts deletes clab-* ports whose netdev is already gone. Deleting the container
250: // side of the veth removes the device and leaves the OVS port in place under that random name.
251: func (n *ovs) removeMissingAutoPorts() error {
252: 	if n.Cfg == nil || n.Cfg.ShortName == "" {
253: 		return nil
254: 	}
255: 
256: 	c := goOvs.New()
257: 	ports, err := c.VSwitch.ListPorts(n.Cfg.ShortName)
258: 	if err != nil {
259: 		return err
260: 	}
261: 
262: 	for _, port := range ports {
263: 		if !clabAutoPort.MatchString(port) {
264: 			continue
265: 		}
266: 		missing, err := ovsInterfaceDeviceMissing(port)
267: 		if err != nil {
268: 			return err
269: 		}
270: 		if !missing {
271: 			continue
272: 		}
273: 		log.Debugf(
274: 			"Removing stale OVS port %q from bridge %q",
275: 			port,
276: 			n.Cfg.ShortName,
277: 		)
278: 		if err := c.VSwitch.DeletePort(n.Cfg.ShortName, port); err != nil {
279: 			return err
280: 		}
281: 	}
282: 
283: 	return nil
284: }
285: 
286: func bridgePortSet(c *goOvs.Client, bridge string) (map[string]struct{}, error) {
287: 	ports, err := c.VSwitch.ListPorts(bridge)
288: 	if err != nil {
289: 		return nil, err
290: 	}
291: 
292: 	set := make(map[string]struct{}, len(ports))
293: 	for _, port := range ports {
294: 		set[port] = struct{}{}
295: 	}
296: 
297: 	return set, nil
298: }
299: 
300: func setOVSEndpointID(iface, endpoint string) error {
301: 	out, err := exec.Command(
302: 		"ovs-vsctl",
303: 		"set",
304: 		"Interface",
305: 		iface,
306: 		"external_ids:"+ovsEndpointExtID+"="+ovsMapValue(endpoint),
307: 	).CombinedOutput()
308: 	if err != nil {
309: 		return fmt.Errorf("tag OVS interface %q as %q: %w (%s)", iface, endpoint, err, out)
310: 	}
311: 
312: 	return nil
313: }
314: 
315: func ovsInterfaceNames(condition string) ([]string, error) {
316: 	out, err := exec.Command(
317: 		"ovs-vsctl",
318: 		"--format=csv",
319: 		"--no-headings",
320: 		"--data=bare",
321: 		"--columns=name",
322: 		"find",
323: 		"Interface",
324: 		condition,
325: 	).CombinedOutput()
326: 	if err != nil {
327: 		return nil, fmt.Errorf("find OVS interfaces: %w (%s)", err, out)
328: 	}
329: 
330: 	text := strings.TrimSpace(string(out))
331: 	if text == "" {
332: 		return nil, nil
333: 	}
334: 
335: 	return strings.Split(text, "\n"), nil
336: }
337: 
338: func ovsInterfaceDeviceMissing(iface string) (bool, error) {
339: 	out, err := exec.Command(
340: 		"ovs-vsctl",
341: 		"--data=bare",
342: 		"get",
343: 		"Interface",
344: 		iface,
345: 		"error",
346: 	).CombinedOutput()
347: 	if err != nil {
348: 		return false, fmt.Errorf("get OVS interface %q error: %w (%s)", iface, err, out)
349: 	}
350: 
351: 	return ovsDeviceMissing(string(out)), nil
352: }
353: 
354: // ovsDeviceMissing reports whether an OVS interface error column says the netdev is gone.
355: // A live interface returns "[]".
356: func ovsDeviceMissing(errorColumn string) bool {
357: 	errorColumn = strings.TrimSpace(errorColumn)
358: 
359: 	return strings.Contains(errorColumn, "could not open network device")
360: }
361: 
362: func ovsMapValue(value string) string {
363: 	if value == "" {
364: 		return `""`
365: 	}
366: 	for _, r := range value {
367: 		switch {
368: 		case r >= 'a' && r <= 'z', r >= 'A' && r <= 'Z', r >= '0' && r <= '9':
369: 		case strings.ContainsRune("-_.:@", r):
370: 		default:
371: 			return fmt.Sprintf("%q", value)
372: 		}
373: 	}
374: 
375: 	return value
376: }

Deleting the container\n// side of the veth removes the device and leaves the OVS port in place under that random name.\nfunc (n *ovs) removeMissingAutoPorts() error {\n\tif n.Cfg == nil || Reasoning
Now let me look at the base versions of these files, plus relevant helpers: `SanitizeInterfaceName`, `SetNameMACAndUpInterface`, `NewEndpointBridge`, `genRandomIfName`, `GetRandIfaceName`, and callers of `RemoveOwnedInterface`, `Endpoint.Remove`, `DeleteBridgePort`.

Key concerns to investigate:

1. `clabAutoPort` regex `^clab-[0-9a-f]{8}$` — check `genRandomIfName()` format. If the random name generator produces e.g. `clab-XXXX` with different lengths or includes other chars, the regex may not match. Let me check.

2. `removeMissingAutoPorts` is invoked during `AddLinkToContainer` via `DeleteBridgePort`, and `Delete`. It enumerates all bridge ports and runs an `ovs-vsctl get` per matching port — O(n) exec per port. During deploy, AddLinkToContainer calls DeleteBridgePort per link → removeMissingAutoPorts lists all ports and runs ovs-vsctl per clab-* port. Performance consideration: for a bridge with N links, each attach costs O(N) ovs-vsctl invocations → O(N²) exec calls. Might be a P3/P4 performance note.

3. `ovsDeviceMissing` string match "could not open network device" — depends on ovs-vsctl output format for the error column. The error column in OVS DB is a set of strings; with `--data=bare` and no `--format`, output might be quoted. Test uses quoted string. Fine.

4. `removeEndpointPorts`: `c.VSwitch.DeletePort(n.Cfg.ShortName, endpoint)` — go-openvswitch DeletePort uses `--if-exists -- ... del-port br port`? Actually the comment says --if-exists covers it. Need to check go-openvswitch behavior: DeletePort does use `--if-exists del-port`. OK.

5. `ovsMapValue` — if endpoint contains characters needing quoting, uses `%q` Go quoting which produces Go string escaping; ovs-vsctl accepts double-quoted strings but escaping conventions may differ (Go escapes with \x, \u etc.). Edge case; topology interface names are restricted anyway. Minor.

6. Concurrency: `AddLinkToContainer` is called concurrently during deploy (parallel node deployment). `removeMissingAutoPorts` may delete ports of other links concurrently being attached? The regex `clab-[0-9a-f]{8}` — random netdev names during deploy. Another goroutine attaching a link could have its port in "missing" state? No—during attach, port is being added; but the race: goroutine A adds port for link named clab-abcdef12; goroutine B concurrently calls removeMissingAutoPorts and sees port clab-abcdef12 whose netdev exists (not missing) so skips. Race window: between AddPort and the netdev existing? The netdev exists before AddPort. So the port's netdev exists. But wait — in `AddLinkToContainer`, `f` renames the link (SetNameMACAndUpInterface). Actually the link exists. Race with `Delete` of the node? Unlikely.

But there's a subtler race: `removeMissingAutoPorts` checks the OVS `error` column. If a container just restarted and its netdev was deleted, another deploy's removeMissingAutoPorts may delete the port while the attach is re-adding... edge.

7. `logicalEndpointName` returns `iface` when the sanitized altname matches. But if topology name is valid Linux name, `SetNameMACAndUpInterface` renames the netdev to the iface name. Check what SetNameMACAndUpInterface does with invalid names — likely uses SanitizeInterfaceName and adds altname. Let me check.

8. In `Endpoint.Remove` (links/endpoint.go): the notfound case now calls `removeBridgePort` — but this runs inside the node's netns ExecFunction. `removeBridgePort` calls `DeleteBridgePort` on ovs node which executes `ovs-vsctl` (which talks to ovsdb over socket, namespace-independent) — fine. But note `removeMissingAutoPorts` inside a netns — fine too.

Potential issue: In `Remove`, when the link is found and deleted, `removeBridgePort` is called. But wait — for ovs bridge endpoints, is the endpoint's netdev actually deleted by LinkDel when it's enslaved to OVS? Deleting a veth that is an OVS port: OVS removes the port automatically when netdev disappears (OVS watches for netdev removal and removes the interface from the bridge... actually OVS has its own datapath; when a netdev disappears, ovsdb-server doesn't automatically remove the Interface row immediately—actually OVS does handle netdev removal; the Interface row stays with error column set, and the Port row remains). That's the premise of the PR.

Now a correctness concern: `Endpoint.Remove` notfound path — previously returned nil. Now calls removeBridgePort which for the ovs node runs `DeleteBridgePort(ifaceName)` → `removeEndpointPorts` (ovs-vsctl find by external_ids) + `removeMissingAutoPorts`. This runs on every destroy for every endpoint of every node — only when node implements bridgePortDeleter (ovs only). OK.

But there's a concern: `removeBridgePort` in `RemoveOwnedInterface` notfound path: the check `node.(bridgePortDeleter)` — nodes is an interface; ovs implements DeleteBridgePort. Fine.

9. `Delete` in ovs: iterates endpoints, calls DeleteBridgePort per endpoint; each call → removeEndpointPorts (2-3 ovs-vsctl execs) + removeMissingAutoPorts (ListPorts + per clab-* port an ovs-vsctl get). Then a final removeMissingAutoPorts. So O(E * (P + E)) execs. For a large OVS bridge with hundreds of ports, this is quadratic number of ovs-vsctl invocations. Each ovs-vsctl invocation forks a process and talks to ovsdb — on the order of tens of ms. With 100 endpoints → 100 * (100 gets) = 10k execs ~ could take minutes. This is a legit P3 performance concern (destroy path). Note: it's per-DeleteBridgePort call, so removeMissingAutoPorts runs once per endpoint, plus once more in Delete. Hmm, that's the design to handle ordering. Could instead collect tagged names once. P3.

10. In `AddLinkToContainer`, `DeleteBridgePort(logical)` — if logical == "" (link nil? no, link re-read; logicalEndpointName returns name if no match). logical is only "" if link==nil which can't happen after LinkByIndex success. OK.

11. `ovsMapValue` used in `find Interface external_ids:containerlab-endpoint=...` — if the value contains spaces it's quoted with Go %q. ovs-vsctl find condition parsing: the value must be a quoted string. Go's %q escapes non-ASCII with \u which ovs may not parse. Interface names are ASCII. Minor.

12. The regex `clab-[0-9a-f]{8}` — need to check `genRandomIfName`. Let me grep.

13. AltNames: does `SetNameMACAndUpInterface` add altname of the original topology name? Let me check links helpers.

14. Test file: uses `clablinks.NewEndpointGeneric(nil, "x", nil)` just to get a random name — a bit hacky but fine. The test requires root + ovs-vsctl; skips otherwise. It creates bridge "br-ovs-leak" on the host ovs — if ovs isn't running, AddBridge fails with t.Fatalf rather than skip. LookPath passes if binary installed but service not running → test fails. CI likely doesn't have ovs running; but skip conditions only cover binary presence. Could be a P3: test fails in environments with ovs-vsctl installed but ovsdb not running. Hmm, but is this test run in CI? Let me check. Also `n := &ovs{}` without Init of DefaultNode... they call `n.Init(&clabtypes.NodeConfig{ShortName: bridge})` which sets n.DefaultNode. OK.

Also `AddLinkToContainer` in test: `clablinks.SetNameMACAndUpInterface(second, ep)` renames the veth to the endpoint name (ixr-dut-30-e1-10, 16 chars — invalid as Linux name? IFNAMSIZ is 16 including NUL, so max 15 chars. "ixr-dut-30-e1-10" is 16 chars → invalid). So SetNameMACAndUpInterface must sanitize to clab-* and add altname. Then logicalEndpointName returns "ixr-dut-30-e1-10" and setOVSEndpointID tags it. Good.

Let me check `SetNameMACAndUpInterface` and `SanitizeInterfaceName`.

15. `removeEndpointPorts` uses external_ids find — but also DeletePort for the plain endpoint name first. If the endpoint name is a valid Linux name, the port is named that. If not, DeletePort with --if-exists is a no-op, then find by external_ids catches the clab-* port. Good.

But one issue: `setOVSEndpointID` is called with `iface = link.Attrs().Name` (the netdev name, e.g. clab-1f57ad50), tagging external_ids:containerlab-endpoint=<logical>. But ovs-vsctl `set Interface <iface>` uses the interface name, not the port name. The Interface row name is the netdev name. OK.

But `ovsInterfaceNames` finds Interface rows by external_ids — returns interface names. Then it checks those names against bridge port set (port names). For OVS, port name == interface name for regular ports (non-bond). Fine for veth.

16. `removeMissingAutoPorts` deletes ports matching `clab-[0-9a-f]{8}` whose netdev is missing. Risk: a user's unrelated bridge with a port named clab-xxxxxxxx that is a real (existing) netdev is kept (only missing ones deleted). But what about non-clab-created random ports? The bridge is containerlab's own bridge named after node ShortName. Deleting "missing" ports on that bridge is scoped to the bridge. But what if the OVS bridge node name collides with a user's pre-existing bridge (containerlab deploys ovs-bridge nodes... the bridge may pre-exist per docs "ovs-bridge kind attaches to existing bridge"? Let me check docs: ovs-bridge kind — containerlab creates the bridge? Actually in containerlab, ovs-bridge nodes create an OVS bridge. If bridge pre-exists (ovs-bridge topology uses existing bridges too?). Check docs/manual/kinds/ovs-bridge.md. If the bridge pre-existed with user ports that happen to be named clab-[0-9a-f]{8} and have missing netdevs (unlikely), they'd be deleted. Low risk.

Also more relevant: with the mgmt-net or user topologies, ports attached by older containerlab versions to the same bridge name... The `Delete` also runs `removeMissingAutoPorts` which will delete ANY missing clab-* port on the bridge — including ports from a *different running lab*? Bridges are per-lab named by node ShortName, e.g. "bridge1". A bridge name could collide across labs (same name in two lab files running concurrently — OVS bridges are system-global). If two labs share an ovs bridge name, deleting stale missing clab-* ports could remove the other lab's... no, only missing ones (netdev gone). Other lab's live ports aren't missing. OK.

But wait — during `Delete` of one lab, another lab's containers could be mid-attach: their netdev exists. Fine.

17. Now the interesting one: `removeMissingAutoPorts` deletes missing clab-* ports even when this is a *redeploy of a different lab using the same bridge name*? Only missing ones. OK.

18. `EndpointGeneric.Remove` — the found-and-deleted path now also calls removeBridgePort. But if the netdev delete succeeded, OVS port may still linger under random name; the tag find covers tagged ports. Old versions (ports created before this change) aren't tagged — handled by removeMissingAutoPorts (missing clab-*). Good.

19. Check: does `DeletePort` in go-openvswitch handle `--if-exists`? Let me check the library... it's a vendored dependency; the comment says so. Previously the same call was used, so fine.

20. Potential nil issue: `n.Cfg` nil check in removeEndpointPorts and removeMissingAutoPorts; DeleteBridgePort calls removeEndpointPorts then removeMissingAutoPorts — both guard. OK.

21. `logicalEndpointName` builds alt map and loops endpoints — per link attach; fine.

22. Race/lock: `AddLinkToContainer` calls `n.DeleteBridgePort(logical)` → removeMissingAutoPorts may delete ports concurrently added by parallel attaches of *other* endpoints on the same bridge? Scenario: goroutine A just called AddPort(clab-abc) but the netdev... exists. Not missing. But what about the ordering: in AddLinkToContainer, `f` (SetNameMACAndUpInterface) runs, then DeleteBridgePort, then AddPort. During DeleteBridgePort of A, another goroutine B's port is live. Fine.

Actually there IS a race: A's netdev is renamed by `f` from clab-1f57ad50 to the final name (or sanitized clab-xxx). Wait — when the iface name is valid, the netdev is renamed to the topology name, so the random name disappears. If OVS still had an old port under the old random name (from a previous deploy), the netdev is now gone → port is "missing" → removeMissingAutoPorts cleans it. That's the fix's purpose.

Race: two nodes deploying concurrently attaching to the same bridge; A lists ports; B's port just added and netdev present → skipped. Then between check and delete nothing else... only deletes ports it determined missing. A port could become missing between the `get error` check and DeletePort if its container is torn down concurrently — that's fine/desired anyway.

But here's a real hazard: A port's netdev could be *temporarily* missing during the attach sequence? In AddLinkToContainer order: rename (f) happens BEFORE AddPort. So between rename and AddPort, the old port (if any) had stale netdev name → missing. If B's removeMissingAutoPorts runs at that moment it deletes A's OLD port — desired. Then A adds new port. OK.

23. Now check `DeleteBridgePort` being invoked from `removeBridgePort` while inside the node's netns (ExecFunction). `ovs-vsctl` exec inside netns — ovs-vsctl uses unix socket, fine. But `removeMissingAutoPorts` uses goOvs library which also uses ovsdb socket. Fine.

24. Docs: is there a doc change needed? The PR changes destroy behavior for ovs. Check docs/manual/kinds/ovs-bridge.md for anything about ports. Probably fine. Also release notes? The repo has .cursor/rules/release-note-review.mdc — let me read it. It might require release-note updates for user-visible behavior. It's 472 bytes. Let me read.

Let me now gather: genRandomIfName, SanitizeInterfaceName, SetNameMACAndUpInterface, NewEndpointBridge, base versions of the files, release-note rule, and callers of Remove/RemoveOwnedInterface.
Now let me look at supporting helpers and the base versions, plus callers of the changed functions.

24: func (e *EndpointBridge) Verify(ctx context.Context, p *VerifyLinkParams) error {
25: 	var errs []error
26: 	err := CheckEndpointUniqueness(e)
27: 	if err != nil {
28: 		errs = append(errs, err)
29: 	}
30: 	// the bridge of a management network that does not exist yet is only known
31: 	// once the network is created, which happens before the links are deployed

45: 		}
46: 	}

48: 		return errors.Join(errs...)
49: 	}

55: }
56: 
57: func (e *EndpointBridge) IsNodeless() bool {
58: 	// the mgmt bridge is nodeless.
59: 	// If this is a regular bridge, then it should trigger BEnd deployment.
60: 	return e.isMgmtBridgeEndpoint
61: }
62: 
63: // CheckBridgeExists verifies that the given bridge is present in the
64: // network namespace referenced via the provided nspath handle.
65: func CheckBridgeExists(ctx context.Context, n Node) error {
66: 	return n.ExecFunction(ctx, func(_ ns.NetNS) error {
67: 		br, err := netlink.LinkByName(n.GetShortName())
68: 		_, notfound := err.(netlink.LinkNotFoundError)
69: 		switch {
70: 		case notfound:
71: 			return fmt.Errorf(
72: 				"bridge %q referenced in topology but does not exist",
73: 				n.GetShortName(),
74: 			)
75: 		case err != nil:
76: 			return err
77: 		case br.Type() != "bridge" && br.Type() != "openvswitch":
78: 			return fmt.Errorf(
79: 				"interface %s found. expected type \"bridge\" or \"openvswitch\", actual is %q",
